# Changelog

All notable changes to this project should be documented in this file.

The format is intentionally simple:

- `Added` for new features
- `Changed` for behavior or workflow changes
- `Fixed` for bug fixes
- `Docs` for README, guide, or release-note changes

## [Unreleased]

### Added
- **Preview Settings now sets the resident viewport's wireframe and vertex-marker colours, beside the background and grid.** The untextured solid renders blue-grey and the topology overlay drew close enough to it that a solid-plus-wire display melted together. The colours already existed, but only behind the Edit Mesh colour buttons, so a reader who was previewing rather than editing could not reach them. `d3d11_wire_color` and `d3d11_vertex_color` sit on the same Gizmo tab, persist with the other viewport colours, and ride the presentation-quality payload that already carries the grid colour, so no new helper protocol capability was needed. Defaults reproduce the renderer's own `MeshOverlayColors.Default` exactly, only a changed colour is pushed through (the host republishes after every accepted frame), and a colour picked inside Edit Mesh still outranks Preview Settings for the rest of the session, the way an in-viewport background choice already does.
- **Edit Mesh now has persistent geometry layers and a mesh-internal selection clipboard.** Copy captures complete selected faces with their UVs, normals, tangents, skin weights, material routing, source maps, and native extra attributes; Paste creates one undoable `Selection copy N` layer while preserving separate material/source fragments internally. The new LAYERS panel supports rename, visibility, ordering, deletion, and active-layer isolation. Base mesh is permanent, hidden copies remain saved but are excluded from output, geometry and layer generations autosave atomically after 750 ms, and Modify Original can resume compatible drafts for the exact source-asset SHA-256 without loading or deleting incompatible work.
- **Edit Mesh selection can target Vertices, Wires, or Faces, with separate customizable committed and live colors.** Click, Brush, Rectangle, and Lasso honor the chosen target; the overlay preference format moves to v3 while retaining v1/v2 wire and vertex choices, and the selected colors now reach the D3D11, GDI, and WPF render paths.
- **Morph & Refit now has per-garment fitting controls for cloth and hard parts.** After binding, select one or more garment parts and apply an Enabled toggle, 0-200% follow intensity, Surface or Rigid mode, and 0-5% clearance measured against the driver's bounding-box diagonal. Surface is the existing flexible barycentric behavior; Rigid transports each vertex in its bound triangle's orthonormal local frame so armour and other hard pieces do not inherit triangle stretch. Clearance runs after either mode and pushes a vertex outward when the moving body would penetrate it, including at 0% follow. Each application is one undoable resident edit, different bound garments can keep different settings, Reset and Bake rebase them, and existing bindings default to Enabled, Surface, 100%, and zero clearance.

### Changed
- **Fresh and resumed Edit Mesh sessions now open in Orbit, and whole-part viewport picking is disabled.** Runtime camera, tool, element selection, and Undo history are intentionally not restored from drafts; Parts & Routing/PARTS is the only whole-part selection surface, while element selection works without a part filter inside the active geometry layer. Switching tool pages inside the same live session still preserves the active tool.
- **Selection strokes now use one correlated, latest-wins background transaction instead of per-dab native work and history.** Each gesture keeps at most one active and one pending update, carries all unsent swept coverage, retains newer provisional geometry across stale acknowledgements, and creates one history entry only when its final response arrives. Lasso completes provisionally on mouse-up, topology waits for the gesture to drain, and a failed worker restores the pre-stroke selection instead of leaving partial history.
- **Subdivide/Refine now applies the shared 200,000-faces-per-submesh limit throughout the resident path and operates on the complete selected region.** Faces subdivide directly; Wires and Vertices expand to incident faces and remap back to split wires or original/generated vertices. Predicted overflow is rejected before mutation with the submesh and counts, while native Undo/Redo restores geometry and selection together in one step.
- **The main workspace strip now follows the kind of work being done instead of mixing full editors into asset and utility groups.** The visible order is Assets, Mesh Editor, Placement & Animations, Texture Upscaling & Editing, and Tools. Assets keeps Archive Browser, Model Library, and Icon Creator; the texture group spells out Texture Workflow, Texture Replacer, Texture Recolor, and Texture Editor; and Tools now holds Retrofit/Repackage, Format Explorer, Translations, Research, and Text Search. Mesh Editor and Placement remain direct workspaces without an extra nested tab bar, saved and detachable-tool navigation still targets the same tool keys, and Format Explorer's location paths use the new visible labels.
- **Edit Mesh binds dense Morph & Refit garments without scanning every body triangle for every vertex.** The resident native core now uses an exact deterministic bounding-volume hierarchy while preserving the same closest-triangle and tie-breaking result. On the synthetic 100 x 100 regression mesh, binding tests 211,065 triangle candidates instead of an exhaustive 196,020,000 (928.7x fewer) and took 0.018 seconds instead of 2.21 seconds; the candidate and driver-triangle counts are carried in the native refit report so the acceleration is guarded without a timing-dependent test.

### Fixed
- **Edit Mesh now opens its resident editing controls instead of leaving only the two preview panes visible.** The shared preview host and Mesh Editor tab were assigning scene request IDs and generations from separate counters, so a placement refresh could make the helper reject the following `mesh_edit` transition as stale. Shared authoring transforms and package-load restoration now use the tab's single latest-wins scene lane; preview-only sessions keep their existing independent scene state.
- **Archive Browser's extension picker now has its complete list the first time it opens after startup.** The first bounded archive page marked the browser ready before the separate extension-facet response arrived, which enabled **Select** while its data was still empty and made the dialog substitute a zero-count `.dds` placeholder. **Select** now remains unavailable until the current extension counts are published, then enables from that queued facet delivery without delaying the first archive page.
- **Opening Mesh Replacement Builder with a very large mesh no longer starves interface updates while its background preflight measures bounds.** The million-vertex scan now yields at the cancellation checkpoint it already visits every 4,096 vertices, so progress, cancellation, and repaint work can run while the worker continues instead of waiting behind one long pure-Python loop.
- **External-model material audits and direct-DDS preview packaging now keep declared evidence authoritative.** Embedded FBX texture references outrank unbound same-role companion filenames, and an explicit material subtype (including specular and glossiness) cannot be reclassified as occlusion merely because a temporary directory contains `ao`, so audit rows, generated roughness maps, and fallback notes are deterministic.
- **Preview texture preparation no longer leaves its source image locked on Windows.** The resize and legacy PBR-split paths now decode images from in-memory byte buffers, and the shared material combiner explicitly closes its Qt file device after decoding, so Python 3.14 can clean up or replace each source immediately after packaging.
- **Solid (Textured) loads its textures in Mesh Editor and in the Viewport section's Preview mode, instead of drawing flat until the mode is swapped back and forth.** The display mode reaches the resident helper down two channels: the narrow `viewport_display_update` the Mesh view control sends, and the presentation snapshot, which is republished after every accepted scene frame. That snapshot carries the whole Preview Settings quality payload beside the mode, including `use_textures_by_default`, which is "load textures automatically after geometry" and is off by default. The helper applied the mode first and the quality payload second, so every republish turned textures back off under a mode whose entire meaning is that they are on. Picking the mode again re-textured the scene until the next frame landed, which is what made it look random. A payload that names a display mode now lets that mode own the textures; `use_textures_by_default` stays the authority only for a payload that names no mode, which is the archive preview's own toggle. Both directions are gated, because dropping the flag outright would have stranded that toggle with nothing to catch it.
- **X-Ray draws the wireframe and vertex colours chosen in Preview Settings instead of always overriding them.** X-Ray draws topology through the surface, where the default black wire is unreadable, so the renderer substituted a fixed high-contrast palette. That substitution was unconditional, which made a colour chosen in Preview Settings look ignored the moment the display mode became X-Ray. The automatic palette is now the fallback for an untouched preference only, and the decision lives in `MeshOverlayColors` alone: the D3D11 viewport held its own fixed copy of it, and the resource metrics reported the constant rather than the colour actually drawn, so the evidence agreed with itself while the viewport drew something else. The GDI and WPF paths already routed through the shared helper and follow automatically. The headless renderer proof asserts both directions, because a fix that simply dropped the fallback would leave an untouched preference drawing black on dark.
- **A background action refused because another task is still running now leaves an error line in the live log, and in the archive log when the action was showing archive progress.** The shell's utility runner reported that refusal through the status field alone. The status field holds one transient line and the logs hold the record, so a refused action produced no trace anywhere a reader could go back to: the log simply ended at whatever the previous step had written. That is exactly how the Modify Original draft-check chain below looked like it had stopped for no reason, with the archive log ending at `Checking Modify Original drafts for <asset>...` and nothing after it. The line names the action that was dropped, so a dropped step is distinguishable from a queued one, which the deferring variant of the same runner already reports as `Waiting for the current background task to finish before starting the next step.` The archive-log decision follows the caller's `show_archive_progress` argument, because the refusal returns before the runner takes ownership of archive progress.
- **A freshly opened Mesh Editor is camera-only, instead of selecting whole parts or grabbing a gizmo handle on the first left click.** The Part Pick toolbar checkbox was removed but its widget was left checked and merely hidden, so every presentation snapshot still reported `part_pick_enabled: true` and MeshViewport kept routing a plain left click into `BeginSelectionDrag(..., "source")` outside `mesh_edit` mode. The placement gizmo defaulted on beside it, and while it is visible a click on a handle begins a placement drag through `TryBeginPlacementGizmoDrag`. Both now open off: a left drag orbits, Parts & Routing is the whole-part selection surface, and element selection stays inside Edit Mesh. Neither control was removed, so Part Pick and the visible Gizmo checkbox still turn their behavior back on, and the Gizmo checkbox now persists under `ui/mesh_alignment/gizmo_visible` exactly as the Grid checkbox already did, so off is what an unset preference reads as rather than a state forced on every open.
- **Archive Browser's Modify Original reaches Edit Mesh again instead of stopping at the draft check.** The new draft-fingerprint pass runs as its own background task and started workspace preparation from that task's completion handler, but Qt delivers a worker's `completed` signal before the thread's `finished` signal, so the shell still saw a task in flight and refused the second stage as a concurrent action. That refusal only reached the status field, so the archive log ended at `Checking Modify Original drafts for <asset>...` with no error line and nothing opened. The chained stage now waits for the shell to go idle, the way every other two-stage archive action does. Resuming a saved draft hid the fault, because that dialog's nested event loop let the first thread finish before the second stage started.
- **Wire + Vertices no longer draws solid fill, and opening Morph & Refit no longer reloads the viewport.** Preview-mode translation has one owner, Morph reuses the native submesh counts it already computed, and page activation limits redraw suppression to the tool column so source parsing, geometry upload, device/surface identity, helper PID/HWND, camera state, and presentation generation stay unchanged.
- **Long Brush and Lasso selections no longer disappear behind delayed resident updates.** Target-specific provisional overlays remain visible until their correlated authoritative response, stale replies cannot clear a newer tail, and completion converges without the old per-dab queue and repeated history churn.
- **Edit Mesh and static replacement now preserve exact PAC skin rows for proven OBJ round-trips.** When the matching `mesh_roundtrip_manifest_v2` sidecar supplies an authoritative source-vertex map, rebuild copies each original influence row instead of estimating weights by surface proximity. Skinned PAC OBJ imports without that sidecar now stop with recovery guidance, and PAC output is rejected if a rebuild changes vertex-record bytes outside the declared position, normal, or UV0 edits.

### Docs
- **`docs/architecture.md` draws the system instead of only describing it.** Seven diagrams cover the process topology and the three single-route authorities, the allowed and rejected import edges, the facade-and-owner split that most of the document's sections are instances of, the resident preview session's states with its two failure lanes, the runtime cache tree, the archive mutation pipeline, and the latest-wins worker contract. The preview lifecycle gained a section of its own, because it was previously spread across a package-map bullet and two paragraphs under Services.
- **The README names the workspaces the app actually shows.** The main strip has been Assets, Mesh Editor, Placement & Animations, Texture Upscaling & Editing, and Tools since the navigation was reorganized, but the feature table still read `Image Editor`, `Recolor Variants`, and `Placement & Animation Studio`. A diagram of the strip and its groups sits above the table, the Mesh Editor row names the selection, geometry-layer, and morph-refit work added in this release, and the helper diagram now lists `cdmw_archive_accelerator` and marks `cd_hkx` as the Rust project it is rather than a fifth C++ one.

## [0.11.0-alpha.3] - 2026-08-03

### Added
- **The resident viewport's grid is customizable: color, spacing, and line count, beside the background color.** The grid turned out to be CPU-built line lists with per-draw colors rather than anything shader-compiled, so the pipe was three presentation-quality fields away: `d3d11_grid_color` (minor lines; major lines draw the same colour lightened by the fixed palette's own +35 step), `d3d11_grid_spacing_scale` (0.1-10x on the scene's spacing), and `d3d11_grid_line_count` (4-40 lines each side of the origin). All three sit in Preview Settings on the same tab as the viewport background, persist with it, and apply live through render tuning; the defaults reproduce the fixed grid exactly.
- **Finish Edit Mesh can no longer be stranded.** Three independent guarantees now stand behind the one button whose failure locks the reader inside Edit Mesh. In the helper, a pending Finish resumes from whichever message clears its last blocker — command results included, where before only a fresh Morph & Refit state update could resume it, which quiet sessions never send — and a four-second fallback timer forces the save outright if the message it waits for was lost, trading at worst an uncommitted procedural morph value for an exit that always happens. On the host, a Finish refused because the action worker or a live stroke was mid-flight retries itself the moment that work drains, instead of silently requiring the reader to click the button again — which is exactly how "Finish does nothing" reports happen.
- **The resident viewport's background color is a Preview Settings choice now.** The renderer has accepted an `#RRGGBB` background through its presentation-quality payload all along; nothing ever sent one. `ModelPreviewRenderSettings` carries `d3d11_background_color` (defaulting to the renderer's own dark grey), the Gizmo tab of Preview Settings offers it as a "Viewport background" color button next to the gizmo colors it shares a panel with, and a change applies live through the same render-tuning lane as the lighting sliders.
- **Selecting is instant now: Brush, Rectangle, and Lasso edit mesh vertices while PARTS remains the explicit whole-part control.** Brush projects each editable part once per drag, bins its vertices into screen cells, and updates the vertex overlay on every pointer move while native depth resolution runs at the bounded protocol cadence. There is no dense-mesh cutoff, and Toggle records a crossed vertex once per gesture so painting back over it cannot silently undo the first pass.
- **Edit Mesh has brush and lasso selection, not just the rectangle.** The Selection combo has offered "Brush Select" — as its first entry and its default — for as long as it has existed, and the resident editor ignored it: every Select drag was a rectangle. The combo's three modes are real now. Brush paints the selection under the cursor as you drag, Blender's Select Circle in spirit: each throttled dab is an add (or subtract, when the operation says so) `screen_brush` selection that the native core resolves with full camera and depth awareness, so the swept path unions into one selection without holes — coalescing that kept only the latest cursor sample would have left gaps, which is why intermediate dabs apply inline off the action-worker path and only the drag's final dab records the selection-history unit. Replace starts a new selection on the first dab, the brush ring shows the paint radius (the Radius control drives it), and a plain click keeps the precise 14-pixel click pick rather than stamping a full-radius dab. Lasso sends the actual swept polygon — the native core has resolved lasso polygons all along; nothing ever sent one — with the rectangle endpoints kept beside it as the fallback older cores read. Rectangle stays exactly as it was, and part-pick drags in placement keep rectangle semantics whatever the combo says.
- **A session can now be watched from four sides at once, including the pixels, so "it flickers" becomes a timestamped list of what blinked and what happened either side of it.** Nothing here had ever recorded an interaction, so a report that the preview reloads arrived with no way to reconstruct the sequence that produced it, and every investigation started by guessing at the click that came first. `scripts/monitor_mesh_editor_session.py --all` now launches the app from source, records what you did, captures the window, and writes one correlated timeline. The capture is the part that could not be inferred: it grabs the window with `PrintWindow(PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT)`, which was measured against the real Mesh Editor viewport and does return the D3D11 swap-chain content, works while the window sits behind other windows, and photographs nothing but the app — so a recording never sweeps up whatever else is on screen. Blinks are found by a rule that separates the two things a log cannot: a region that changed and *came back* within a few frames is flicker, while content that changed and stayed is the interface working, and only the first is reported. Capture holds itself to a quarter of wall clock and drops its own frame rate to stay there, because `PrintWindow` drives the application's UI thread and a monitor that slows the thing it measures is measuring itself; a 1220x800 window costs 23ms a frame and the tool prints the rate it actually achieved. The in-app half is a Qt event filter recording clicks, focus, and which widgets repainted, hid and showed — widgets hiding is called out on its own, because a pane that hides collapses the splitter beside it and resizes the embedded viewport. It is inert unless `CDMW_SESSION_RECORDER` is set, keystroke text is redacted unless `CDMW_SESSION_RECORDER_KEYS=1` is passed as a separate deliberate act, and its trail is written to a subdirectory rather than the crash reports folder, because `latest_diagnostic_report_files()` globs that folder into the shareable diagnostic bundle and a record of every click does not belong in a file attached to a bug report without thinking about it.
- **The Mesh Editor's viewport can now be a node inside Qt's own scene graph, rather than a Win32 child window embedded in it.** The 3D view has always been a separate window parented into a Qt widget, and that seam is where the editor's positional defects came from: it stayed parented to a window Qt had destroyed on a monitor move, learned about resizes by polling and then waited for them to stop, and needed an elaborate hidden-window reveal to avoid being watched mid-assembly. A child window is also always topmost inside its own rectangle, so no tooltip, popover or overlay could ever be drawn across the model. `native/cdmw_qt_rhi` is a working `QQuickRhiItem` that renders through Qt's D3D11 RHI as an ordinary scene-graph node, with the interface composited above it in the same frame. It is a proof of concept — it draws a test triangle, and porting the mesh renderer onto it has not started — but it establishes the whole path end to end. It is C++ rather than Python because PySide6 renders through `QQuickRhiItem` perfectly well and then never lets the process exit, which is worse than the flicker it would replace. `scripts/build_qt_rhi_plugin.ps1` builds it in one command; `scripts/vendor_qt_sdk.py` fetches the exact Qt that PySide6 ships, verifying every archive against the SHA-256 Qt publishes beside it, because the plugin uses `QRhi` from Qt's private module and Qt gives no compatibility promise across versions there. The build fails outright if the two ever disagree, since a mismatch does not announce itself: it corrupts somewhere else later.
- **The Item Finder has a Stats section, and it says which slot an item equips to.** Finding a piece of armour by name and then learning anything about it was not possible: the detail pane listed evidence, names, materials, models and icons, but nothing the game itself would call a stat. `EquipTypeInfo` is now decoded — 113 rows, each a hash key and a slot name, all 113 distinct — and an item row is matched against it. The match is safe to trust because every one of those keys is above 2^24: a value equal to one is that key, not an ordinary small integer that happened to collide. 2,973 of the catalogue's 3,624 entries name exactly one slot, and the mapping is what you would expect — boots to `Foot`, gloves to `Hand`, cloth armour to `Upperbody`, and Abyssal Dragon Armor to `DragonArmor`. A row that names two slots reports none, because a guess is indistinguishable from a fact once it reaches the pane. The 651 entries with no slot at all are consumables and quest items, and the section says so rather than sitting empty. The slot also joins the search text, so typing `helm` finds every helm; descriptions deliberately do not, because folding prose into the index would make one item's flavour text match half the catalogue. `EquipTypeInfo` is the first table in `cdmw/core/table_catalog.py` marked `runtime_usable`, and only for the two fields actually proven against the corpus. A Description section sits beside Stats and shows the text recovered earlier in this release, which until now reached the resident catalogue but never the dialog that displays items.
- **Every item in the Item Finder now carries its in-game description.** Each `iteminfo` row holds a second localization key beside its display-name key, in the same shape and one id later, and nothing in the app had ever read it — all 6,508 rows carry one. Exact row boundaries made it free to pick up: 3,574 catalogue entries now have the description text the game shows, and it travels with the row through the resident catalogue and the native indexer alike.
- **Middle-drag and right-drag are rebindable camera gestures now, in Model Preview Settings > Controls.** Each can pan (the default) or orbit, independently; holding the scroll wheel to pan — or to orbit, if that is your habit from another editor — is a choice rather than a fixed rule, and the orbit/pan modifiers stay as they were. Whatever the two buttons are bound to, pan and orbit both remain reachable through the left button's modifiers, so no combination can lock the camera. The navigation strip along the bottom of Edit Mesh and the controls hint both name the drag buttons alongside the modifiers they share a move with, in every language. This fix also revived middle- and right-drag panning itself: the viewport's mouse-move handler treated "left button not held" as "gesture over" and cancelled the pan on its first move, so holding the scroll wheel had done nothing at all.
- **The Archive Browser now plays the sounds a Wwise `.bnk` embeds.** A bank lists its embedded sounds in a `DIDX` table and holds them in the `DATA` chunk that follows, and the bundled vgmstream reads them as subsongs — the preview pane reads that table, offers one row per sound labelled with its Wwise source id, and decodes the chosen row through the same decoder that already serves a loose `.wem`. The chooser only appears when a bank holds more than one sound, each sound caches to its own WAV so switching rows cannot serve whichever was decoded first, and a codec the decoder cannot read costs that one sound rather than the whole preview. A bank with no `DIDX` keeps its readable analysis instead of an empty player, because its audio streams from separate `.wem` files and there is nothing inside it to play. `.bnk` is registered as playable with a `wav` export in `schemas/archive_content_capabilities.v1.json`, and Export as WAV takes the selected sound.
- **A catalog that is translated but still mostly English now fails a gate instead of shipping.** The key-set contracts could not see the problem that put 451 half-English strings into German and European Spanish, because every key was present and every value was non-empty. `tests/test_localization_translation_quality.py` measures the share of each catalog's multi-word strings where most of the English words survive into the translation, and fails above 2.5%; healthy built-ins measure 0.3-1.0% and the two damaged ones measured 6.8% and 7.6%. The failure names the worst offenders with their source and translation. It runs in the `responsiveness` area.
- **CDMW Full can switch its complete interface among 14 built-in languages without restarting:** English, German, European and Latin American Spanish, French, Italian, Brazilian Portuguese, Polish, Russian, Turkish, Japanese, Korean, Simplified Chinese, and Traditional Chinese. The saved choice covers startup, shell and feature dialogs, model-backed text, accessibility/help text, and the resident .NET Mesh Editor; live changes preserve open-window state and the loaded mesh. Version-2 custom packs add CLDR plural branches while version-1 and partial overlays remain compatible. A generated 9,495-key Python/C# presentation manifest now requires an explicit valid entry in every built-in catalog, and packaging rejects stale or structurally incomplete catalogs before PyInstaller.
- **The camera's orbit and pan modifiers are rebindable, in Model Preview Settings > Controls.** Orbit defaults to Alt or Ctrl and pan to Shift; either can move to Alt, Ctrl, or Shift alone. The viewport tests pan before orbit, so a pair sharing a key would leave orbit silently dead — picking one moves the other off the clash and the dialog says which binding it changed and why. Middle-drag and right-drag always pan and are deliberately not rebindable. The strip along the bottom of Edit Mesh names whatever the two are bound to now rather than a fixed pair of keys.


- **Translation Studio can produce translations, not only edit them.** The panel could already rewrite any of the game's 187,521 lines by hand, which is the wrong tool for more than a handful of them — so "Translate with AI" sends the lines you have filtered to a model on **your own API key** and writes the replies into the same edit map a hand edit uses. A machine translation is highlighted, revertable and exported by the same button as everything else.
- **Five providers, three request shapes, one base URL field.** Anthropic (`/v1/messages`), OpenAI, any OpenAI-compatible endpoint (OpenRouter, DeepSeek, Groq, Together, LM Studio), Google Gemini, and a local Ollama/LM Studio model that needs no key at all. "OpenAI-compatible" is one preset rather than eight because they all speak `/v1/chat/completions`. **No OAuth**, deliberately: a ChatGPT or Claude *subscription* login is not an API credential, and neither provider issues API keys through a public OAuth flow, so a "sign in with…" button would promise something that cannot work.
- **The key is encrypted to the Windows account** via DPAPI, so the file in the workspace is useless to another account or on another machine. Where DPAPI is unavailable the key is stored plainly and the dialog says so — silently pretending otherwise would be worse than the warning.
- **Every reply is checked against its source before it is applied.** `<br/>` appears 23,751 times in the English table, `{Key:Key_Skill_1}` 824 times, `{Money:Money_Copper:1}` 246; a model that renders `{Key:Key_Roll}` as `{Tangent:Key_Roll}` produces a line that reads fine in the editor and shows a literal brace-string in the game. Same tokens, same count of each, or the line is reported and left as the game shipped it. `[EMPTY]` is protected as a sentinel; `[Effect]` is not, because that one is prose the player reads.
- Batches carry a numeric id per line, so a reply can arrive incomplete or reordered and still land on the right rows, and each batch is applied **as it lands** — Stop after twenty minutes leaves twenty minutes of translation in the table. 429 and 5xx retry with backoff honouring `Retry-After`, with the sleep sliced so Stop still answers; a bad key is not retried, because it will not get better. Scope is never implicit: the dialog lists the selected line, the lines on screen, the whole search and the whole table with counts, and asks again above 2,000 lines.

- **The Prefab Inspector can now add and remove objects.** Right-click an object heading: *Duplicate this object* / *Remove this object*. The previous entry built the resizer; this is the part a modder can reach. Object rows carry their group's byte offset, and `locate_element` turns that into the collection element behind the row by exact offset match — not containment, because an object nested *inside* an element is not itself one, and offering to delete it would delete its parent.
- Structural changes apply **immediately**, unlike every other edit the Inspector makes. Path and value edits are pending and keyed by byte offset; a splice moves every byte after it, so a pending edit would then land on the wrong field — and on a field that still looks like a plausible target. So the dialog keeps the bytes it opened with alongside the bytes the current rows were read from, *Undo all changes* restores the first, the tree is rebuilt by re-decoding rather than patched, and a structural change is refused outright while row edits are pending, with a dialog saying to save or undo them first.
- Removing the last element of a collection is offered but disabled with the reason on its tooltip. Refusing in the menu explains itself; refusing after the click does not. The hint above the tree mentions the row menu, because a feature only reachable by right-click is a feature nobody finds.
- Driven through the real dialog over **60 shipped prefabs**: duplicate an object, then *Undo all changes*, and the payload is byte-identical to the file as opened — 60 of 60.

- **`.prefab` collections can be grown and shrunk.** `cdmw/core/prefab_array_edit.py` adds `duplicate_prefab_element` and `remove_prefab_element`, which splice a whole element body in or out, write the new count, and repair everything the splice moved. The previous entry validated the byte-moving machinery by shrink-and-regrow; this is the count-writing step it said was still missing.
- The primitives are *duplicate* and *remove*, not *insert a new element*. An element body's layout depends on that element's own member mask, so there is nothing to synthesise from — but a copy of a sibling is by construction valid for the same collection, and retargeting it afterwards through the path rewriter is what the edit was for in the first place.
- The hazard worth ruling out was the 8-byte owner preceding every pointer record: if those held absolute positions, each one after a splice would need fixing. Measured over 1,500 shipped prefabs, an owner is either `NULL_OWNER` or a small ordinal, and **not one of the 706 non-null owners fell inside the data blob**. They are indices, so the splice does not touch them.
- Validated the only way it can be, since no two shipped prefabs differ by exactly one collection element: **duplicate then remove the duplicate, and demand the original bytes back. 687 of 687 round trips are byte-exact, none differing.** Every result is also read back before it is returned and refused unless the walk completes, the collection carries the new count, the file is the size the splice implies, every pointer still satisfies `value == offset + 4`, and the copy reads back the same size as its source. That last check is not redundant — one corpus file passed all the others while its re-walk resynchronised a few bytes inside the copy.
- `PrefabDocument.collections` now records each collection's header offset, header width, declared count and every element's byte span. None of this is recoverable after the fact: a header is only distinguishable from the bytes around it by having been arrived at, and an element ends wherever the previous one stopped being read.

### Changed
- **Mesh Replacement Builder and Edit Mesh give the model more room and keep side controls readable at narrow widths.** The Builder no longer repeats the resident camera legend, selection strip, placement-review banner, preview-ready/performance rows, or a full-width footer while Edit Mesh is active; Cancel and Build Mod now stay with the control column. Export transforms put X/Y/Z inside three distinct numeric fields instead of squeezing separate axis labels between them. Edit Mesh removes its embedded status footer, keeps status and the widened/inset FPS counter in the pane headers, and uses denser fields, buttons, colour swatches, section spacing, lists, and side-panel type without removing any tool.
- **Mesh View and Edit Mesh now have one honest textured mode.** `Solid + Wire` was visually indistinguishable from `Faces + Wire` whenever its texture request failed, so it has been removed from both controls; `Solid (Textured)` is the single textured choice. Saved preferences and protocol messages carrying the old `textured_wire` value remain compatible and resolve to `Solid (Textured)` rather than resetting the view.
- **Morph & Refit is a guided workflow instead of a technical form.** Create Profile opens four pages — Profile, Parts, Deformation, Preview & Save — generates stable IDs automatically, lets the reader check named parts or use the acknowledged viewport mesh selection captured when the wizard opened, and offers Volume, Scale, Move, Flatten, Taper, or Twist with only the relevant axis fields visible. Advanced holds category, generated ID, range, feather, falloff, mirror, and local basis. Minimum/default/maximum previews are correlated with the resident session; Finish saves the existing v2 format and returns preview to zero, while Cancel resets and removes the temporary definition. The main section is linear: profile and sliders, optional Refit, Review and Apply. Refit names its driver and garment selections and refuses overlap; Reset and Bake stay explicit, and saving never bakes.
- **Edit Mesh separates mesh-region selection from whole-part selection.** Click, Brush, Rectangle, and Lasso in the viewport select mesh vertices; a whole part is selected only through PARTS. Every new editor opens on Orbit with no selection armed, operation Add, and shape Brush; operation and shape stay sticky while switching tools. Move and topology commands accept either the viewport selection or an explicit PARTS selection. Sculpt tools use the current selection, or establish an internal initial-hit scope without changing the PARTS selection.
- **Selection mode defaults to Add.** A mesh selection is built up across several gestures far more often than it is restarted, and Replace threw the previous region away on every one of them.
- **The viewport's background and grid colors are picked in the Viewport section, where they are seen.** Preview Settings has carried a "Viewport background" color since earlier in this release, which is the wrong place to stand when the thing you are matching is in front of you. The Viewport group now has Background and Grid swatches beside the wire and vertex ones, with a Reset for the pair, saved next to the topology appearance in `mesh-editor-viewport-background.json`. A color chosen here outranks the host's presentation snapshot rather than being overwritten by the next accepted scene frame.
- **Static mesh and LOD parsing is 33-58% faster.** The `.pam` and `.pamlod` decode paths in the Archive Browser preview and the Mesh Editor asset loads ran one `struct.unpack_from` call per index and up to five per vertex, plus a Python-loop smooth-normal pass that dominated large static meshes. The index read, the sorted-unique vertex gather, the face remap, and `_compute_smooth_normals` now run as vectorized numpy passes with the scalar loops kept as the authoritative fallback, held bit-identical by `tests/test_pac_vectorized_decode_equivalence.py` and by a 409-file real-game comparison across `.pam`, `.pamlod`, and `.pac`. Measured against the installed game: a 43.8 MB debris `.pam` parses in 2.26s instead of 3.39s, a 21 MB breakable in 0.81s instead of 1.57s, and the three largest `.pamlod` files dropped from 0.69-0.91s to 0.36-0.49s.
- **Legacy-mode archive reads no longer spawn a helper process per file.** Every raw entry read outside the v2 prepared flow ran the native archive accelerator first, and that path costs a subprocess launch with a temp-directory JSON job and report round trip per entry, measured at 25-45ms against sub-millisecond in-process decodes. Worse, the accelerator reports encrypted entries as unsupported, so the game's ChaCha20-encrypted files paid the full round trip and were then decoded in-process anyway. The in-process decode now goes first (its LZ4 and ChaCha20 already run through C-backed packages), and the accelerator remains as the fallback for payloads this process cannot decode. Measured against the installed game: 80 real reads, half encrypted, average 6.2ms per read through the public path where the accelerator-first order averaged about 30ms, with byte-identical output confirmed on a stratified 182-entry sample.
- **The first click on a model after a fresh archive cache prepares its dependencies about 4x faster.** The archive worker's prepare batch ran strictly sequentially: each of up to 4,096 entries awaited its raw-source hash, decode, and publication before the next began, so a monster `.pac` whose preview needs 108 prepared files spent 1.56s in preparation on a cold prepared cache. Preparation is per-entry work with no cross-entry state, the native decode is stateless per call, and the protocol writer already serializes concurrent messages, so `PrepareManyAsync` now runs its entries with bounded parallelism (up to eight at once) while keeping request-ordered results, first-failure semantics, and the content-addressed source-hash contract exactly as they were. Measured headlessly against the installed game: the 108-file cold batch fell 1.56s to 0.39s, a 28-file batch 0.29s to 0.09s, and warm batches 0.07s to 0.02s because the per-hit source re-hash parallelizes the same way.
- **Model previews build 3-5x faster once the archive cache is warm.** Opening a `.pac` in the Archive Browser preview, and loading the same asset into the Mesh Editor or Edit Mesh, was dominated by pure-Python per-vertex work: `parse_mesh` unpacked every 40-byte PAC record with six `struct` calls, the preview model re-derived vertex normals one triangle at a time, and every texture re-ran semantic inference across every sidecar binding. The hot paths now decode in vectorized numpy passes, with the original scalar loops kept as the authoritative fallback and held bit-identical by `tests/test_pac_vectorized_decode_equivalence.py`; sidecar semantic rows and reference normalizations are cached per text, and a model's decoded HKX collision document is reused across preview rebuilds. Measured headlessly against the installed game with a warm v2 catalogue: a 24.4 MB monster `.pac` with 108 prepared dependencies builds its full preview in 3.2s instead of 13.9s, a 5 MB wagon model in 0.9s instead of 4.3s, and a 15-file sample suite (five each of `.pac`, `.dds`, `.paloc`) fell from 21.5s to 5.9s of total preview build time.
- **The selected part's detail readout is structured and colour-coded instead of one grey paragraph.** The Parts group's detail is now captioned rows: the selection summary in strong text, the vertex and triangle weight beneath it, then — for a single part — a Material row in a warm tone, a Texture row in a cool one (still only when it differs from the part's own name), and a salmon Hidden badge when the part is switched off. Same data, same local reads; the eye just no longer has to parse a run-on line to find the material route.
- **Subdivide and Refine Smooth from the resident rail require an explicit mesh or PARTS selection on both surfaces.** The embedded dialog and Mesh Editor host now reject an empty selection instead of silently densifying the whole editable mesh, and the rail's Topology help text states the requirement before the command runs.
- **The Part Pick checkbox is gone; part picking is simply always on.** Both of them: the builder toolbar's checkbox and the editor's left-side Part Pick section. The Parts panel is the selection surface, and the toolbar toggle mostly confused: ticking it also happened to trigger the lazy texture resolve, which read as "Part Pick loads textures for some unknown reason". Hover highlight and the right-click part menu are always available in placement now.
- **Resizing the window during Edit Mesh no longer resurrects the right-side setup panel.** The responsive-layout pass runs on every dialog resize and unconditionally showed the setup column — a column Edit Mesh deliberately hides. It now respects the edit mode it already knew about.
- **Subdivide and Refine Smooth no longer widen an empty selection to the whole editable scope.** Select mesh vertices in the viewport or choose whole parts under PARTS first, so topology density cannot change outside the region the user explicitly chose.
- **The archive progress bar is written only when something changed.** `_set_archive_load_progress` runs at progress-callback cadence and re-entered Qt's native style machinery on every call — including re-arming the indeterminate busy animation with `setRange(0, 0)` when it was already indeterminate, and re-writing an identical value. The one recorded full freeze wedged the main thread inside `QProgressBar.setValue` with no Python frames below it; skipping redundant writes narrows that exposure and removes a steady source of pointless repaints. The freeze's root cause is still open — it needs a native stack (procdump/WinDbg) captured while wedged, which Python tooling cannot see past the Qt frame.
- **The session monitor stops counting its own reflection, and stops reporting the empty fault log as a crash.** The repaint numbers that read as a storm — tens of thousands of "paint bursts" with nothing changing — were dominated by native-window (`QWindow#...`) repaints that track the frame capture's own rate almost one to one: `PrintWindow(PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT)` forces every native window in the app to re-render once per captured frame, so the monitor was measuring itself. The report now counts widget repaints and native-window repaints separately and says so when a capture was running. The zero-byte `native_fault_current.log` the app truncates at startup is also no longer announced as a fresh crash report ~1.4s into every session; it is announced the moment it gains content.
- **Edit Mesh's scene inspector shows what the selected part is made of, and stops spending height on labels.** The Parts list was names alone, so "how heavy is this part" and "which material does it route through" meant leaving the editor; each row now carries its vertex count, and the group below the list reports the selection, its vertices and triangles, and — for a single part — its material route and texture. Every one of those was already on the .NET side of the protocol, so this reads local state rather than asking the host for anything. Select All, Clear and Invert join Show/Hide, Duplicate and Delete. The "SCENE" band above the column is gone: Parts, Action History and Viewport name themselves, so it only cost height. So did the "?" badge on every section — it was pinned inside the caption row, which meant each section reserved that row's height even where the caption is blank, putting a dead band above every tool's first field. The help text is unchanged and now hangs off the section itself.
- **Opening an Edit Mesh tool is one step instead of a visible sequence.** Each click re-measured the preferred width of the whole scene inspector and of every page and row button, moved the splitter, re-laid out three containers and invalidated the flank, with nothing suppressing the repaints in between — so the column was seen to hitch, resize and then fill. The measurements are cached per page and only discarded on a font or DPI change, which are the only things that can move them; the whole layout pass is wrapped in the existing redraw batch; and a row already on screen no longer triggers a scroll-into-view, which was re-laying out the scroll host on most clicks.
- **Fixed: switching between two Edit Mesh tools that share a page left the settings under the tool you came from.** Move and Grab share the Transform page and the three brushes share Brush, and the sync that follows the armed tool returned early when the page had not changed — so arming Inflate after Smooth highlighted Inflate while its radius, strength and falloff stayed open above it, under Smooth. The open body now follows the armed row, not just the page.
- **The Item Finder no longer shows or filters by material, because the classification was never consistent enough to be worth trusting.** Material tags were recovered by matching model names against `PartPrefabDyeSlotInfo` dye slots, which is a name-shaped guess rather than a field the item table states. It was right often enough to look plausible and wrong often enough to mislead: an item could carry a tag belonging to a different part, carry several contradictory ones, or carry none while an identical-looking sibling carried three. Getting it to 100% means decoding the dye-slot and mesh-group tables properly and proving each link against the corpus, which is a large piece of reverse engineering for a facet nobody was choosing items by. That is not worth the time, so the honest move is to stop showing it rather than keep a filter whose results cannot be defended. The **All materials** dropdown, the **Materials** detail section, the material tags in the legacy Finder's tooltips and evidence, and the material clause in both search placeholders are all gone, and material tags no longer contribute to search text — an invisible match on unreliable data is worse than no match, because the reader cannot tell why a row appeared. The `material_tag` filter, the material facets, and the row's material list are removed from the item catalogue contract on both sides rather than left dangling. Material evidence remains on the underlying index, where it still feeds Archive Browser scope filters and the Material Authority tools that were built for it; only the Item Finder stops presenting it. Item **categories** are untouched: an item whose category is Material is still a crafting material and still filterable as one.
- **Edit Mesh's tools and their settings are one column now, and no tool is named twice.** The rail armed a tool and the page it opened then offered the same tool again: arming Inflate on the rail lit a second Inflate button inside a page headed "BRUSH", and Move/Grab and Select did the same on their pages — five of the six tools existed twice on screen at once, with two controls for one piece of state. The rail and the property panel are now a single list where each tool is a row and the open tool's settings appear directly beneath the row that opened them, so the row *is* the page's header and there is nowhere for a duplicate to live. The header that used to say "BRUSH" while you held Inflate is gone with it, and the duplicated button rows are deleted from the Selection, Transform and Brush pages; Grow and Shrink stay, because they are commands rather than a second way to pick the tool. Topology, Colour and Morph & Refit keep one row each under a "COMMANDS" heading that marks where arming stops and one-shot commands begin. Opening a row's settings never re-parents a realised control tree — the pages stay in one host and only the host's cell moves — so the embedded-window failure that page reveal has to guard against cannot be reached by expanding a row.
- **The Edit Mesh dock is only as wide as what is open in it.** Both columns reserved fixed widths: 74 logical pixels of rail plus 340 of property panel, a flat 414 whether a tool was open or not, and 336 for the scene inspector — the same at 1920x1080 as at 3440x1440, so the two clamp helpers that looked like they scaled the dock with the window were dead code that only the layout gate called. Widths are measured from content now: 196 with nothing open, 357 with a brush open, and the scene inspector bounded to 260-340 by what its rows actually need. Closing a tool hands the width back to the viewport instead of leaving a full-height empty panel. Morph & Refit is the one page allowed past the 420 ceiling, up to 620, because its Author Slider… / Save Profile / Delete Profile row clips rather than wraps.
- **The Edit Mesh brush now shows the falloff curve it is about to apply.** "Smooth", "Linear" and "Constant" named a shape the reader had to know already; the Brush page draws the profile across the brush diameter, with strength scaling its height. The curve is a port of `brush_falloff_weight` from native mesh core rather than a drawing of one, so it cannot disagree with what a stroke does — the port lives in `BrushFalloffProfile`, names the C++ file it mirrors, and the headless layout gate asserts its values against the formula. Radius deliberately does not reshape the curve, because the profile is normalised over the radius; the radius is named on the preview's caption instead.
- **Edit Mesh's left rail is one flat list of tools now, and the Classic layout is gone.** The rail used to show six category buttons — Select, Move, Brush, Topo, Colour, Morph — where clicking a category revealed its page *and* armed that page's default tool, so picking "Brush" always meant Smooth until you found the real brush buttons inside the page. Every armable tool is now its own rail button — Select, Move, Grab, Smooth, Inflate, Pinch — and clicking one arms exactly the tool it names; Topo, Colour and Morph keep one entry each that only opens their command page and never touches the armed tool. Revealing and choosing are fully separate: no page reveal, whether from a rail entry, a layout re-activation, or the host re-asserting a state, arms anything anymore. The Classic side-panel Edit Mesh layout and its toggle buttons are removed outright — the tool rail is the only Edit Mesh layout, and leaving mesh edit returns just the placement panels (Part Pick, Placement, Viewport) the way they were built. The classic-only left-edge navigator strip went with it. "Live Alignment Preview" spent a whole-width line naming what the user was already looking at, and "Drag axes/center to move; Alt-drag to rotate; wheel zooms." spent another restating the tooltip; both rows now belong to the viewport. The two buttons that shared the title row moved into the preview controls row, which Edit Mesh already hides — so inside Edit Mesh the tool rail's own actions are the only ones on screen.
- **Format Explorer's "Where to edit it" column now gives the exact path in this app, spelled the way the interface spells it.** "Texture Workflow", "HKX editor" and "Material sidecar editor" were descriptions rather than names — none of them matched a tab or menu entry anywhere on screen. Every location is now a path of real labels ("Assets > Mesh Editor", "Archive Browser > Edit Material Values...", "Placement & Animation Studio > Driven bones", "Archive Browser > Import WAV + Patch to Game..."), each segment is translated exactly as the tab or action it names so the path matches the screen in every language, and a test checks every segment against the shell sources — renaming a surface now breaks a test instead of leaving a stale promise. Filtering no longer lags or flickers: four auto-sizing columns re-measured on every one of the ~500 cell writes a refresh makes, and the shrinking selection re-rendered the detail pane for each row it crossed on the way; a refresh now repaints once, sizes columns once, and changes selection once.
- **Weapon Placement Studio is removed from the Archive Browser.** It had been a disabled "WIP" stub for long enough: a greyed-out button in the preview toolbar's Maintenance menu, a greyed-out context-menu entry, a dead button in the Asset Family dialog, and a mixin whose only act was to print "disabled". All four are gone, along with their four catalog strings in every language. Weapon Placement *Batch* — the living feature with a similar name — is untouched, and the placement workflows themselves continue to live in the Placement & Animation Studio.
- **Translation Studio no longer freezes the window while a language loads, and shows a progress bar instead.** Reading a 16-25 MB table off the archives already ran on a worker, but parsing its 187,521 entries — twice, when a reference language is shown alongside — ran on the UI thread in the loaded handler, which is what the multi-second hang on Load was. The parse now happens on the worker and the handler only installs the finished catalogue. A progress bar beside the Load button runs while a load is in flight, and the first-time language sweep drives it with real counts, one tick per package table swept.
- **The Translation model dialog now describes providers neutrally.** The Anthropic preset's note ranked the Claude models — "strongest", "the balance most translation passes want", "cheapest" — and the local-model preset warned that a local model "translates the game's tone far less reliably than a hosted one". A settings dialog is not the place for a vendor's sales copy, ours included: both notes now state only where the key comes from and where the current model list is documented, and the model choice stays the user's.
- **Right-clicking a folder in the Archive Browser's file list now offers Export Folder...**, which extracts every file in that folder and the folders below it into a destination you pick, keeping their structure. Folders previously had no context menu at all — the right-click handler returned unless the row was a file — so the only route was to select the folder and use Extract Selected, which was neither obvious nor, as it turns out, working.
- **The Archive Browser's Folders view expands and scrolls without the second-long pause it took on every step.** Listing a folder's children ran a pass over all 1,674,781 entries of the archive, decoding and classifying each into a full entry record just to read its path, then threw away everything but the 256 rows it returned — so a five-child folder cost the same as the root, and every 256-row page inside a folder paid it again. The index is sorted by path, so a folder's entries are one contiguous run: the backend now binary-searches that run and walks only it. Where a category filter, an active sort, or the package-root hierarchy rules that out, the scan remains, but it reads paths instead of building entries. Measured on the real game archive: expanding the root 1219 ms to 0.4 ms, a leaf folder 1018 ms to 0.1 ms, scrolling 1,536 rows of a 25,511-file folder 6.2 s to 59 ms, and the package-root folder picker 1762 ms to 797 ms. Names inside a folder now sort the way the index itself does, which places `_` before a letter — the order Flat view was already showing.
- **The file list no longer rebuilds every visible row's text on every repaint.** The v2 model formatted a row's eight columns and eight tooltips inside `data()`, which a view calls once per role per column per visible row per frame: 2,412 rebuilds per repaint for 42 visible rows, about a fifth of the paint. They are cached per entry now, as the model this one replaced already did. Twenty scroll repaints of a full folder fell from 1005 ms to 739 ms.
- **Edit Mesh opens with the camera in your hands, not the Select tool.** The tool rail picks the page that owns the viewport's current tool, then that page asserts its own default tool. Edit Mesh boots on `orbit`, which no page owned, so the fallback landed on Selection and armed Select: the first drag on a model you had only wanted to look at changed the selection instead. Orbit — and any tool the rail has not been taught — now owns no page at all, so the rail opens with nothing highlighted and nothing armed, and the tool-properties column collapses to just the icon rail until you pick a tool. The camera has no rail button of its own; it is always on the left button and on the modifiers, which is what the bottom strip is for.
- **Every Edit Mesh tool now shows the camera keys that still work while it is active.** A brush or Select owns the left button, so the only way to turn the model without dropping the tool is a modifier — and nothing on screen said so, because the label carrying those bindings was only ever added to the viewport in the simple-preview profile and Edit Mesh runs the authoring one. A permanent strip across the bottom of the editor names the active tool and then the live bindings: hold to orbit, hold to pan, wheel to zoom. The key badges are highlighted exactly while an edit tool holds the left button. Alt is new as an orbit override; Ctrl already worked and still does.
- **The Placement & Animation Studio opens in about a second instead of six, and drops far fewer frames doing it.** Three loaders ran on the UI thread as the window came up, and the largest of them — the motion-clip index, a walk of all 33 package tables for 316,059 `.paa` files — had no on-disk cache at all, so it was rebuilt on every open. It is cached now, and the stored rows carry each clip's category, because re-deriving that costs fifteen regex probes per path and was 0.66s of the 0.9s it now takes to load. The index is also built when the Clips tab is first opened rather than at startup, the way the two rig tabs already defer their archive walk; the features that read clips from outside that tab ask for it themselves. Measured open-to-quiescent on one install: 6.03s to 1.59s, and the event-loop turns long enough to drop a frame fell from 39 to 6 — which is what "low FPS for the first few seconds" was. Reading the cache is still one ~250 ms turn, so this is a large reduction rather than a clean sixty frames a second.
- **A character's weapon socket files and action charts are no longer re-extracted on every open.** 140 archive entries and 33 MB of decompression, paid again on every character switch, for files that only change when the game does. Stored as one 3.6 MiB blob they read back in 0.04s. What gets extracted is now decided by the pinned baseline alone rather than also by what the edit session happens to hold, so a character switch made after an edit can no longer store a partial chart set as if it were the whole one.
- The first Studio open after this change is a slow one — around ten seconds rather than six. Widening the signature changes its shape, so the *existing* wearables index no longer matches its stored key and is rebuilt alongside the two new caches. It is paid once per install, and once more after each game patch.
- **Both new caches are keyed on the `.paz` archives as well as the `.pamt` tables, and re-check that key after the read that filled them.** A cached row is a location — an offset and a length into a `.paz` — so signing only the tables would accept a cache whose payloads had been rewritten underneath it, and computing the key after a five-second walk would stamp data read from the old install with the new one's identity if the game patched mid-scan. Timestamps are compared in nanoseconds rather than whole seconds. A walk that skipped an unreadable package, or a read that lost files, is shown but never stored: cached, that gap would be served on every later launch with nothing left to retry it. The clip index additionally keys on a digest of the classification patterns, so editing a rule without renaming its label is a cache miss instead of 316,059 rows keeping the old answer.
- **Settings > Performance now describes the caches the app actually has.** The preview cache controls still carried their pre-Vortice wording. "Aggressive" advertised that it "prebuilds a few nearby visible models" — that prefetch went away with the resident migration and `_start_archive_native_preview_prefetch` has been a no-op ever since, so the mode is a larger budget and nothing else; it says so, with the numbers (512 MB of packages and 192 MB of decoded textures at Balanced, 2 GB and 512 MB at Aggressive). "RAM cache" is now "Remembered previews", because a model preview lives in the resident renderer and what the list holds is a small reference to its durable package, not the payload the old label was sized against. The orphaned `native_preview_package_prefetch_limit` is gone with the promise it backed.
- **The remembered-preview count is disabled when the disk cache is Off, instead of quietly doing nothing.** Only a preview backed by a durable package can be remembered, so with the .NET/Vortice disk cache off the count had no effect at any value — an invisible dependency between two adjacent controls. Both surfaces that expose it, Settings > Performance and the Preview Settings dialog, now grey it out and say why.
- `ArchivePerformanceSettings.ui_frame_budget_ms` and `.background_worker_limit` are removed. Both were clamped, persisted and plumbed through two dialogs while nothing read them: the frame budget had no consumer at all, and the worker limit lost its spinbox some time ago, so its manual-override branch could never be reached. The legacy `performance/background_worker_limit` key is still read, but only where it already was — as the migration fallback for a pre-split install's sidecar worker count.
- **An AI API key that cannot be encrypted is no longer written to disk.** Storage tries Windows DPAPI and used to fall back to `plain:<base64>`, which is encoding rather than encryption: anyone who could read the file could read the key, and the warning shown at the time did not change that. CodeQL reported it as clear-text storage of a credential and was right. Everything else in the config still saves, the key still works for the session, and the dialog now says it was not saved rather than that it was saved unprotected. On Windows, where DPAPI is available, nothing changes.

- **Placement & Animation Studio is a top-level tab, next to Assets, instead of one of four inside Tools.** It is a whole application — a viewport, seven panes and its own status bar — so nesting it drew a second tab bar directly above its own, and the two read as one strip of unrelated tabs. `_add_lazy_shell_tool` takes an `index` now so a tool can claim a slot rather than only appending; reattaching a detached tool remembers the index it was built at, which it previously derived by counting earlier tools in the same bar — correct for a group bar that holds nothing else, and wrong for `main_tabs`, where it would have put the studio in front of Assets.

- **There is no `.prefab` trailer grammar — the "trailer" is pointee length fields the walk never consumed.** Over the 799 completed walks whose blob ends `<u32> 01`, *every one* of those u32s equals its own distance from a real pointee start: the same relation `_read_pointer` validates mid-file. Controls make it conclusive — the value ±1 explains 0 of 799, and a value borrowed from a different file explains 3.3%.
- So `closes_blob` now **validates** instead of tolerating: every residual byte must be the `01` terminator or a length field naming a real pointee. No spare-byte allowance, no footer of unexplained width. That replaces the four heuristics accumulated earlier in this session and the two before it.
- Those heuristics were all reading one repeating structure through an off-by-one frame: `<u32 length> 01` repeated is indistinguishable from `01 <u32>` repeated if you start a byte later, which is why `01 01 25 00 00 00 01 70 00 00 00` parsed two ways with nothing to choose between them. Tolerating both is what a rule does when it does not know what it is looking at.
- **The validation I had been relying on was worthless, and that is the real lesson.** I justified each widening partly with "the newly-completed files recover exactly the same objects". That is guaranteed in advance: widening a close rule never changes the decoded prefix, only the verdict. It could not have detected a false positive, and the verdict is what switches editing on.
- One unprincipled rule survives, now labelled as such: a bare `5 <= remaining <= 6` allowance earning 23 files validation cannot explain. Validation alone scores 899, tolerance alone 922, **both 930**. Kept because dropping it costs more than it buys; anything that shrinks it is an improvement.
- Corpus fixtures that padded blobs with invented zero trailers now end with the real `01` terminator — they had been encoding a shape no shipped file has.
- **Completion 878 → 930 of 1,500 (58.5% → 62.0%)** across the session, and the resize round-trip sweep 687 over 371 files → **796 over 422, still exact on every one**.

### Fixed
- **Edit Mesh deformations and Morph sliders now commit the same geometry the viewport shows.** The host's screen-selection bridge unconditionally rewrote the helper's `vertex` target to `source`, so Brush, Rectangle, and Lasso selected PARTS and Grab, Smooth, Inflate, and Pinch could deform a broad or invisible target instead of the visible brush region. Screen gestures now preserve element targets and reject part targeting; the initial-hit scope only filters native screen candidates, Move requires an actual selection, and Grab has its own visible radius. After a helper restart, the resident mutation queue also kept stamping edits with the previous process generation, then discarded the successful resync acknowledgement; the renderer correctly rejected those stale edits while the queue later marked its already-applied recovery as failed. Process or session adoption now rebinds the queue, restores its negotiated correlation capabilities, and lets an applied resync acknowledgement clear recovery before pending work resumes. Stale activation acknowledgements are rejected by the shared controller before Mesh Editor can treat them as ready. A separate missing router branch discarded every completed `dotnet_morph` outcome before authoritative geometry and state reached the editor; Morph results now follow the same correlated completion path as other resident strokes. Create Profile commands serialize across the action-worker cleanup handoff, slider updates are paced to one acknowledged plus one latest pending value, and Save, Delete, Reset, Bake, and Finish queue behind active Morph work instead of leaving the workflow stuck.
- **Solid (Textured) now receives the real PAC materials in both Mesh View and Edit Mesh without later reverting or restarting.** Modify Original resolved one material graph but compiled it twice in series — Imported first, Original/reference only after the first renderer acknowledgement — so a real character spent minutes on duplicate synthesis and the textured-mode watchdog expired between the two. Exact clones now compile once and bind the same decoded resources to both scene-role ranges in one acknowledged generation. Packed raw material DDS maps are passed through like raw normal and height maps instead of a deliberately skipped preview decode aborting the entire texture set. The temporary Faces frame stays an effective loading view instead of overwriting the requested Solid mode, including in the resident combo, while genuinely distinct Imported and Original graphs retain independent generations and pane-specific errors.
- **Finish Edit Mesh now closes only after the resident preview has actually returned to placement mode and the Builder controls were restored.** A mode change requested while an authoritative scene-frame worker was active previously returned “queued” without sending any protocol message, and a resident helper could outlive the host's cached frame. The helper now carries its own source identity in `save_request`, giving the interaction-only placement transition an exact recovery correlation; finalization fails instead of reporting `saved` when the Builder shell hook is absent. The matching scene acknowledgement restores the controls without stopping the helper, while a five-second timeout leaves Edit Mesh open with a correlated error.
- **A preview mode picked in Edit Mesh no longer reverts or flashes on the next click, selection or tool.** One older shared-preview path still ran on every hidden Qt tool refresh: it republished a full presentation snapshot initialized as `mode=textured, grid=false`, followed by an identity scene frame, bypassing the Mesh Editor tab's merged desired-state authority. One rail click could send that stale state five times because the same tool refresh was called redundantly. Tool adoption now performs one refresh and publishes only `tool_state`; the grid, display, camera, placement, and decoded textures remain untouched. Temporary texture fallback is likewise excluded from desired presentation state, so later selection and scene publications cannot make Faces permanent.
- **Brush select paints under the cursor instead of lagging a frame behind it in visible steps.** Two separate costs. The instant local echo recomputed, for every dab, both the screen projection of every editable vertex *and* a front-facing test that walked every face of the submesh looking for one containing the vertex — O(vertices x faces) per sample, tens of millions of triangle orientations on a real garment part, on the UI thread, at the 30ms sample cadence. Neither input changes while a drag lasts, so both are now built once per drag and every dab after that is a distance test. Second, the 30ms cadence throttled the echo as well as the protocol message; the echo now runs on every pointer move and covers the segment since the last one, while the message rate stays exactly as it was. The cadence bounds how often the host is asked for an authoritative result, not how often the reader sees the stroke.
- **Solid (Textured) now settles both Original and Imported independently.** The editable package can acknowledge its baked material generation before the lazy Original resources exist, and the old shared completion marker treated that one acknowledgement as success for both panes. Every material generation now carries its internal `editable/imported` or `original_reference` role. Textured mode settles only after every role required by the scene has applied, and failures name Original or Imported while leaving the last valid pane state visible.
- **Lasso and Rectangle select stopped being reset to Brush on the next control refresh.** Two hosts publish `tool_state` into one `selection_mode` field with two vocabularies: the builder's Selection combo sends the drag shape, and the Mesh Editor tab sent its element mode (`vertex`, `face`, `edge`, `part`). The helper records what it last heard so it can tell a real host change from a plain republish, and recording an element mode there made the builder's very next refresh look like a change back to `brush` — combo assignment included. A reader who picked Lasso had it taken away again within the same second, every time, which is why lasso appeared not to work at all. The tab no longer publishes an element mode as a drag shape (it already travels as `target_mode`), and the helper discards anything that is not one of the three shapes before it can reach either the combo or the record. `tests/test_select_drag_shape_survives_tool_state.py` holds both halves.
- **Move, Grab, and sculpt brushes now follow the pointer before the service round trip.** Move renders a transient transform for the selected mesh elements or explicit PARTS selection on every pointer update; Grab, Smooth, Inflate, and Pinch patch renderer-local geometry from the stroke baseline and immutable screen-space candidates. Selected vertices are rendered as GPU point markers instead of four CPU-projected cross vertices apiece, and fixed Move/Grab scopes cache their incident face ranges once while rotating three transient buffers so the renderer never waits on the buffer still being drawn. Grab iterates only its initial weighted vertices and uses its own adjustable radius, while sculpt brushes query 32-pixel spatial buckets instead of scanning the whole mesh each frame. Authoritative samples are bounded to 16 ms, carry all cursor travel since the last publication, and keep the existing one-in-flight plus one-latest-pending dispatcher. Stale stroke IDs or revisions cannot roll the preview back, Cancel restores the baseline, and the matching terminal geometry clears the provisional state after recording one history entry. In corrected 30-second hidden production-D3D11 soaks at 1920×1080/144 Hz over 180,000 vertices, input-to-present p95/max measured 5.87/6.66 ms for Select Brush, 5.87/6.05 ms for Move, 5.90/7.01 ms for Grab, 5.89/6.34 ms for Smooth, 5.89/6.44 ms for Inflate, and 5.89/6.19 ms for Pinch; no frame exceeded 20.83 ms, pending depth never exceeded one, and no measured run triggered a Gen1 or Gen2 collection. The corrected gate counts only vertices whose final authoritative position differs from the stroke baseline; Move changed 45,000 vertices and Grab/Smooth/Inflate/Pinch changed 5,564/11,353/11,360/11,358 respectively. The visible read-only real-PAC gate then selected 13 vertices across 12 projected faces while PARTS stayed empty, moved exactly those 13 vertices through 40 physical pointer positions and 10 bounded protocol updates with an exact terminal endpoint, measured 0.57 ms handler p95 and a 30.25 ms maximum Qt heartbeat gap, retained one helper process with zero restarts, bound 38 archive textures, and left every checked PAMT/PAZ hash unchanged.
- **The Select shape combo and the viewport agreed on "Brush" and behaved as "Rectangle".** `ConfigureCombo` assigns `SelectedIndex` before the handler that forwards it exists, so the combo's own default was never delivered to the viewport, which stayed on its own `rectangle` default until the reader picked something. Both defaults are now brush, and the viewport is told the combo's value at construction.
- **Returning to Mesh Editor from another tab no longer leaves the viewport on "paused while hidden".** Visibility now moves through explicit inactive, resuming, and active states correlated with activation, process generation, and package generation. A matching resident package activates immediately; a different identity loads before activation instead of revealing the wrong scene. One timed activation retry precedes the existing automatic helper recovery and rehydration path, and a recovered cycle receives a fresh retry budget. Twenty hide/show cycles retain the healthy PID and preserve revision, camera, selection, tool, and display state while every `activated` acknowledgement clears the paused overlay.
- **The PAC parser corpus gate no longer flags the six-influence skin decode it was never taught about.** The harness's bone-width rule shipped in July expecting at most four influences per vertex; the August discovery that the 40-byte PAC vertex record packs six (two u32 groups of three 10-bit palette slots plus six u8 weights) rewired the parser but not the gate, so a real-game corpus run reported over 1,600 false `bone_influence_width_too_large` issues across the first 1,200 character models and `--fail-on-issue` could never pass. The rule now bounds against the parser's own `PAC_SKIN_INFLUENCES` constant so the two cannot drift again, with tests pinning that six-wide rows pass and seven-wide rows still fail. Re-scanned 3,200 real character `.pac` entries: zero issues, gate green.
- **A display mode picked on the builder's "Mesh view" combo survives Edit Mesh instead of reverting on the next click.** During Edit Mesh the presentation snapshot overrides the display mode with a remembered per-session slot, and only the resident rail's combo ever wrote that slot — so choosing Solid (Textured) on the Qt toolbar held exactly until any tool click, selection change or accepted frame republished the presentation, which snapped it back to Faces + Wire. The Qt combo's handler now records its pick into the same slot before routing, so the two combos are equal writers and nothing changes the preview mode except the user.
- **Changing the display mode repaints immediately; textures no longer wait for a camera move.** The synchronized display-mode path updated the viewport state, rebuilt the pane records, and then wrote the new mode into the per-pane presentation contexts — in that order, so the rebuilt records still carried the old mode and the next real rebuild only came with a camera move or resize. That stale-pane window is what "the mesh stays grey until I move the camera" was. The context sync is followed by its own pane rebuild and repaint now.
